Why that is safe: nothing between can read the texture (a draw, dispatch, texture copy or clear, event, fence, query, conditional rendering, a clear drawn as a triangle, a clear of other targets while it is attached, another texture object over its memory, a buffer update over its guest memory or the submission's end stops the scan with the store kept), the clear then waits as a load-action clear or lands in a pass of its own over the whole attachment, so every byte the store would have written is overwritten before any read; the texture is in a GPU-only pool (no CPU access), its own storage, not sparse or lazily backed. Not with the profile's `pass_alternate` `<W>x<H>` shapes on (a kept frame turns the later clear into a load; cube shapes have no depth), nor for window-area, clear-only, layered, slice-merged or face-split passes. The common case it cannot prove is the frame's last pass on a depth buffer cleared in the next frame's first submission: the measurement's "not attached again" and cross-submission counts show how much that leaves.
  - (c) `DM_NVN_MSAA_RESOLVE_ONLY`: a Downsample's resolve pass loads the samples and changes none, then stored them again; with the same scan proving the samples cleared whole before any read, it stores the resolve only (`MTLStoreActionMultisampleResolve`).
  - Not done: a store whose next use is a clear in a later submission (the scan cannot see it, and a guess would leave garbage if a read came first); a load before a full-screen draw (coverage depends on the vertex shader).
  - Barriers, `DM_NVN_HOST_BARRIER`: the host's encoders kept `barrierAfterQueueStages:MTLStageAll beforeStages:MTLStageAll`. The after-stages stay All (the producer of what they read, or the reader of what they overwrite, may be any stage); the before-stages are now the encoder's own that touch earlier results: Dispatch | Blit for the compute and copy encoders (`enc_c_begin`, `blit_open`, the one-off copies, the upload batches `up_begin` / `up_flush` and the zeroing batch), Fragment for host render passes whose vertex shader reads nothing the GPU wrote (the clear, texture copy, texture draw, resolve, clear-rectangle, zeroing and one-off passes, and the DLSS alpha pass: `copy.metal` / `draw_tex.metal` / `texdump.metal` `vs` take only the vertex ID, `clear.metal` `clr_vs` a value the CPU wrote). The feedback and geometry passes (`enc_r_begin`, their vertex stage reads GPU results), the draw passes' full barrier and the present keep All. On a compute encoder Dispatch | Blit is every stage it has, so only the render passes change in effect: their vertex work may start before earlier work ends.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_ls_reduce` (Metal validation with `MTL_DEBUG_LAYER_VALIDATE_LOAD_ACTIONS` and `_STORE_ACTIONS`, which fill what a DontCare load or store leaves, encode thread off and on): 14 cases each with its switch off and on read back colour, depth, stencil, the resolve destination and the samples byte for byte equal and at their literal values, with rule (a) firing once (a clear into an open pass; not for a scissored clear or a draw into the target first), rule (b) on both planes, on the depth alone (the stencil kept for a depth-only clear) and past a clear of another depth target, and never with a draw between, a scissored or masked clear, the clear past the command list's end or another texture object over the memory; rule (c) when the samples are cleared next and not when they are drawn over or nothing follows; and the measurement counting a frame's stores cleared unread in the next one. `t_ls_reduce --bench` (2048x2048 colour + D32S8, 200 frames): 20 MB a frame of depth / stencil stores dropped.
- **Mac (M4 Max):** the bench's GPU time (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF=every:10`) is 0.060 ms a frame of render passes with the rules off and on, the ledger's stores 72 → 52 MB a frame: the M4 Max's bandwidth and lossless compression of cleared content hide it, so the phone decides.
- **Expected on the phone:** with `DM_NVN_LS_STATS=1`, the `[ls-stats]` lines give the avoidable share: "cleared or discarded at the next pass on it with no use between" minus its texture-pool part is store traffic no read needs, and "saved by rules" what the three rules took of it; the residency set line's `attachments per frame … store` should drop by the depth / stencil part. A/B: `DM_NVN_DS_DEAD_STORE=0`, `DM_NVN_CLEARED_LOAD=0`, `DM_NVN_MSAA_RESOLVE_ONLY=0`, `DM_NVN_HOST_BARRIER=0` (or `ab` with `DM_AB`) against the default on the same route, on battery: frame rate, the power line, `[gpuprof]` render time. No `[RED]` GPU fault, no depth or picture fault (a dropped store read later would show as garbage depth: z-fighting or missing geometry).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md), iPhone 18 Pro Max, X2.0 (the GPU-traffic audit, NX18-X2.0 open-optimizations.md); not yet measured on a device.

## The system's performance traces know NX 18's state

- **What:** iOS and macOS 27 record Metal performance all the time (frame interval, GPU time and usage, memory) and keep it for days. On the iPhone: Settings › Developer › Enable Performance Trace › Lookback Collection, then the Performance Trace button in Control Center right after a slowdown; the processed trace goes to a Mac, where `metalperftrace overview <trace> --include-state-transitions` (or Instruments) reads it. On the Mac, `metalperftrace collect --last <duration>`.
- **The states:** the host tells StateReporting what it is doing (`dm/host/state_report.m`, `state_report.h`), so the trace splits its metrics by them. Domain `com.nx18.screen`: Gate, Loading (the game's start until its first frame, `hle_lifecycle.c`), Playing, Menu (NX 18's in-game panel, `DmGamepad.m`), Background (`front_ios.m`). Domain `com.nx18.hold`: Frame rate cap, Heat hold 30, Low Power hold 30 (`hle_nvn.c` `thermal_capped`). Each change is also a `[state]` log line. Changes come at the pace of the player's actions, under the system's rate limit.
- **What it settles:** a frame-rate drop with the same state and low GPU usage is the GPU running slowly at a lowered clock, not a heavier scene; a drop that comes with a state change is that state.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/host/tests GAME=<id> build/t_state_report && dm/host/tests/build/t_state_report`.

### The GPU clock stuck at a third of its speed with the phone cool (the clock rescue)

- **Symptom:** Breath of the Wild drops to about 40 fps for a minute or more at thermal state 0, and the log's GPU probe reads about 2.9x the session's fastest; the same scene held 60 before. It comes and goes: another run past the same place held 60, its probe dipping to about 3x for a few seconds and coming back (botw-r2 against botw-r3, X2.0 round, 2026-11-16). The pause menu ended it at once.
- **Cause:** iOS sets the GPU's clock to the frame rate it sees delivered. Late frames lower the rate, the lower clock keeps the same frames late, and nothing NX 18 sends says a 16.7 ms deadline was missed (NX18-X2.0/gpu-clock-collapse.md). The menu's light frames hit 60 again, and iOS raises the clock.
- **Fix:** `clock_rescue_tick` in `hle_nvn.c`, its decisions in `nvn_rescue.h`. A game with a render scale lever (a render-scale stub: Breath of the Wild, Tears of the Kingdom; or the GPU time stretch of the Mario Kart World family: Mario Kart World, Donkey Kong Bananza, Splatoon Raiders) that has been below 90% of its 60 fps target for 2 s, with the latest GPU probe at 2.3x or more, no heat or Low Power Mode hold or movie, and the thermal state below 2 as the heat ladder last read it (`DM_HEAT_FORCE`'s when forced), draws its 3D views at 0.75 of their scale for 3 s, then its factor goes back to 1. The factor goes through the render scale's setter beside the heat ladder's ([The heat ladder's resolution rungs and the clock rescue share one render scale](#the-heat-ladders-resolution-rungs-and-the-clock-rescue-share-one-render-scale)), so neither undoes the other. A rescue that comes right after the last one doubles the wait before the next (10 s, up to 160 s), so a scene that cannot hold 60 at any clock is left alone; a minute at 60 starts the wait again. Each start and end is a `[nvn] GPU clock rescue` line; `DM_CLOCK_RESCUE=0` turns it off. In Bananza (no frame step) the target is its own controller's top rate, and a rescue lets that controller probe back to 60 at once: its tries from 30 at full size failed at the lowered clock. A step the game fixes itself (state +0x24: Mario Kart World's own 30 fps mode, Breath of the Wild's VFR override at 2) is the game's choice, not a stuck clock, though iOS lowers the GPU's clock at that cadence too: the target follows it (60 / step, 30) and it holds the rescue off, so it is never rescued, a rescue under way when it begins ends at once (`… over …: (the game fixed its own step)`), and a minute of it starts the wait again (`nvn_rescue_target`, `nvn_rescue_held`). Thermal state 2 or worse holds it off the same way: iOS lowers the GPU's clock for the heat there, which a rescue cannot raise, and in a game with the resolution rungs the 30 fps hold waits 15 s behind 70% (heat-ladder.md D15), in which a rescue would have stacked its 0.75 under 70% (0.525; ×3.63 in the stretch family) and spent a back-off slot; a rescue under way ends on the first state-2 sample (`… over …: (thermal state 2 or worse)`). Before the resolution rungs no game waited there, and below state 2 the rule is as before. Before this, the rescue also fired in Breath of the Wild's VFR-fixed 30; that it no longer does is intended.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/host/tests GAME=<id> build/t_clock_rescue && dm/host/tests/build/t_clock_rescue` (with the game's own fixed step: no rescue in 700 s of Mario Kart World's 30 fps mode with the probe at 2.5x, a rescue under way ended at once, the back-off started again after a minute of it; at thermal state 2 no rescue in the 15 s before the 30 fps hold, one started at state 1 ended on the first state-2 tick, and none held with no thermal state). Replaying the rule over botw-r2's per-second lines rescues inside the stuck stretch; over botw-r3 it never fires.
- **The cool-down (2026-11-18), the default in the stretch family:** the rescue above held 60 with 0.75-sized views and did not bring Bananza's clock back on the iPhone (5 rescues, the probe at 3.2x through each). The owner's pause did: about 5 s of the menu with the GPU 20-30% busy took the probe from 3.2x to 1.3x, and play came back at 60 (NX18-X2.0 gpu-clock-collapse.md, run 6). So in the Mario Kart World family the rescue cools instead (`nvn_rescue.h` `nvn_rescue_cool`, `hle_nvn.c` `clock_rescue_tick`): the same trigger, the views at the floor (`RESCUE_COOL_SCALE` 0.5: the stretch's ×4) for `RESCUE_COOL_HOLD_S` (8 s; `DM_CLOCK_RESCUE_S` still sets it), the game's controller held at interval 3's band, 20 fps (`hle_fctl.c` `dm_fctl_cool`; no probe, no pin; at its own 48-60 fps in run 7 the GPU stayed 50-80% busy, and at 30 in run 10 45-55%, the clock low after every one; the pause menu that brings it back runs 20-30% busy), then the factor back to 1 and the game's controller probes back to 60 as usual. `DM_CLOCK_RESCUE=classic` keeps the rescue that holds 60, `=cool` asks for the cool-down in a game with a render-scale stub, `=0` turns both off. Lines: `GPU clock cool-down on: …` once, `GPU clock cool-down #N at frame …` and `… over at frame …`. Check: `t_clock_rescue` (its hold, its factor while under way, a `DM_CLOCK_RESCUE_S` hold standing). On the iPhone it failed at every level: held at the game's rate, at 30 and at 20 (the GPU then 22-33% busy, the pause menu's level), the clock stayed low after each. So the pause brings the clock back by something other than its load. **Off by default in the stretch family since 2026-11-18**; `DM_CLOCK_RESCUE=cool` or `=classic` turns one on for experiments. **The clock kick (experiment, `DM_CLOCK_KICK=<ms>`):** the owner found that even an instant pause brings Bananza's 60 back, and runs 10 and 11 show the first frame after the menu at 17-19 ms of GPU and the next at about 10 ms: a near-idle frame or two, then heavy work, and iOS raises the clock at once. So once the clock is stuck, one present holds the game that many ms (the GPU drains to idle) and the controller may try 60 at once (`clock_kick_tick`); back-off 10 s doubling to 160 s. Not yet run on a device.
